# POC Report: Direct Local Executable

## The Question

Can direct invocation of an existing local Go 1.26.9 executable with child-only GOTOOLCHAIN=local keep build, vet and test on that exact version across inherited selection settings and module/workspace suggestions, reject higher language minimums without upgrading, and leave persistent defaults unchanged?

## Verdict

**Confirmed for the approved bounded Windows/local-executable trial.** All ten cases completed with their expected outcomes. Six compatible cases built, vetted, tested and ran under Go 1.26.9. Four higher-minimum cases explicitly rejected Go 1.27.0 requirements without upgrading. Parent environment, persistent settings, candidate identity and generated input snapshots remained unchanged according to the driver checks.

This does not relabel the first trial: `poc-report.md` remains Inconclusive. This report does not prove a portable selection product, generic-launcher bootstrap, absent-version installation, cross-platform discovery or real dependency compatibility.

## Evidence And Scoring

The developer approved this distinct follow-up after the original failure and changed route were explained. One new run, zero retries, zero model calls. The candidate was found by read-only inspection at an existing Go module-cache distribution. Its VERSION file was only a discovery hint; actual version/environment probes, executable hashes and compiled runtime assertions provided the execution evidence.

| Fixture | Parent `local` | Parent `go1.26.9+auto` |
|---|---|---|
| Plain Go 1.26.0 module | Exact-version build/vet/test/run succeeded | Exact-version build/vet/test/run succeeded |
| Compatible module suggesting Go 1.27.0 | Stayed on 1.26.9; succeeded | Stayed on 1.26.9; succeeded |
| Module requiring Go 1.27.0 | Build/vet/test rejected minimum | Build/vet/test rejected minimum |
| Compatible workspace suggesting Go 1.27.0 | Stayed on 1.26.9; succeeded | Stayed on 1.26.9; succeeded |
| Workspace requiring Go 1.27.0 | Build/vet/test rejected minimum | Build/vet/test rejected minimum |

Derived audit counts from the captured events: 56 commands, all completed; 44 exited zero and 12 were expected build/vet/test minimum-version rejections. Ten executable-provenance records agreed. Six test executions and six built-program executions reported runtime=go1.26.9. Generated fixture hashes match the first trial's input hashes exactly.

**Cache limit:** normal Go caches were not cleared. The first three compatible vet traces show the selected distribution's vet.exe. The second parent-setting pass did not show fresh vet.exe execution, consistent with reused analysis results. Successful vet commands are recorded, not a claim that the analyzer ran fresh in every case. Tests used `-count=1`, so no cached test verdict is accepted as a fresh runtime assertion. This is not a cold-cache or performance experiment.

## Run Context And Identity

- Branch/base: `feat/atom-v1-toolchain`, public main `8b4dd51135c3836d5921414878182b16ff2fdefc`.
- Environment: Windows; standard-library-only generated fixtures; Python automation with Atom's existing owned-process runner.
- Child selection: direct candidate executable, GOTOOLCHAIN=local, inherited child GOROOT removed and actual root verified.
- Offline controls: GOPROXY=off; no checksum-policy override, toolchain installation, dependency retrieval, private target access or model call.
- Command deadline: 60 seconds; separate wrapper cleanup guard unchanged. No deadline extension or retry.
- Selected executable SHA-256: `5b25a4a434c7bbba11112a94c3fa9c1502c0f377bd971705ee8e07b8e3c12620`.
- Original driver remains unchanged: `c4053f08e473fefb756712137717b040c65940c4cc512f3050cc786cb14cd73e`.

## What This Changes

| Assumption | Evidence | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| A preinstalled exact Go distribution can be used without the generic launcher's bootstrap | Version/root/hash probes and runtime assertions succeeded | There is a demonstrated route to consider in phase 01 design |
| A newer toolchain suggestion must force an upgrade | Compatible module/workspace suggestion fixtures stayed on 1.26.9 | Exact local execution can ignore suggestions within this bounded route |
| A higher Go language minimum can be silently accepted by a pinned older compiler | All twelve corresponding build/vet/test commands rejected it | Preserve explicit incompatibility; do not silently upgrade or claim validation |
| Parent settings must change to pin a child command | Environment and persistent-settings checks remained unchanged | A child-only design is feasible for this local route |
| A successful compiler-selection experiment fixes actual dependency incompatibilities | Not tested | No such claim; real projects and version compatibility need their own evidence |

## Explicitly Not Covered

- Linux/macOS, architecture variants, other exact versions, adversarial executable provenance or exhaustive cache/SDK discovery.
- A missing/incomplete distribution, network retrieval, installation, trust bootstrap or generic Go launcher behavior.
- Project configuration storage/precedence, per-PR checkout integration, command wrappers, gofmt selection or subprocesses that invoke another Go through PATH.
- Custom Makefiles/build tags, CGO-enabled builds, real dependencies, real PR evidence, race/performance testing or model review quality.
- A fresh analyzer process for every cached vet result; all build inputs being reproducible from the Go version alone.
- Production code implementation or the separate policy for continuing reviews after confirmed tooling incompatibility.

## Recommendation And Gate

Proceed to a phase 01 design discussion only after the developer accepts this report. Use this evidence to describe exact-version project intent, child-only execution, requested/observed provenance and explicit incompatibility. Configuration schema, override rules, missing-version behavior, supported platform claims and integration with existing gates remain design decisions, not proven production behavior.

No new runtime code, configuration schema, installation, commit, push or release is approved by this experiment. Preserve both original and follow-up verdicts independently.

## Completeness

- [x] Question copied verbatim from the approved follow-up brief.
- [x] One executed trial, ten cases and no retries; complete verbatim evidence retained.
- [x] Actual executable/version/runtime controls checked; fixture hashes match original inputs.
- [x] Source/default mutation checks and owned-fixture cleanup completed without a reported failure.
- [x] Cache, Windows-only, discovery and dependency boundaries disclosed.
- [x] Recommendation limited to the next design gate, not implementation.
- [x] Developer approved this bounded result and transition to phase 01 spec in conversation: "Approve report and proceed to phase-1 design spec".
